=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a permutation P of (1,2,\dots,N) and an integer K.  
Find the expected value, modulo 998244353, of the inversion number of P after performing the following operation:

- First, choose an integer i uniformly at random between 1 and N - K + 1, inclusive.
- Then, shuffle P_i, P_{i+1}, \dots, P_{i+K-1} uniformly at random.


What is the inversion number?
The inversion number of a sequence (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) is the number of integer pairs (i, j) satisfying 1 \le i < j \le N and A_i > A_j.


What does "expected value modulo 998244353" mean?
It can be proved that the sought expected value is always rational. Under the constraints of this problem, when this value is represented as an irreducible fraction \frac{P}{Q}, it can also be proved that Q \not\equiv 0 \pmod{998244353}. Thus, there is a unique integer R satisfying R \times Q \equiv P \pmod{998244353}, \ 0 \le R < 998244353. Report this integer R.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
P_1 P_2 \dots P_N

Output

Print the answer in one line.

Constraints


- All input values are integers.
- 1 \le K \le N \le 2 \times 10^5
- P is a permutation of (1,2,\dots,N).

Sample Input 1

4 2
1 4 2 3

Sample Output 1

166374061

The operation changes the permutation P into the following:

- (1,4,2,3) ... probability 1/2
- (4,1,2,3) ... probability 1/6
- (1,2,4,3) ... probability 1/6
- (1,4,3,2) ... probability 1/6

The expected value of the inversion number is \displaystyle 2 \times \frac{1}{2} + 3 \times \frac{1}{6} + 1 \times \frac{1}{6} + 3 \times \frac{1}{6} = \frac{13}{6}.
\displaystyle \frac{13}{6} modulo 998244353 is 166374061, so print this number.

Sample Input 2

1 1
1

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

10 6
7 4 10 5 6 1 8 2 3 9

Sample Output 3

499122200

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's design code:

import sys
MOD = 998244353
INV4 = pow(4, MOD-2, MOD)

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]; K = data[1]
    P = data[2:2+N]
    if K == N:
        ans = (K % MOD) * ((K-1) % MOD) % MOD * INV4 % MOD
        print(ans)
        return
    # compute total_inv and first_window_inv
    bit = [0]*(N+1)
    total_inv = 0
    first_window_inv = 0
    # local vars for speed
    bit_arr = bit
    n = N
    # define inner functions? We'll inline? 
    for i, v in enumerate(P):
        # sum
        s = 0
        idx = v
        while idx > 0:
            s += bit_arr[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        total_inv += i - s
        # add
        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit_arr[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx
        if i == K - 1:
            first_window_inv = total_inv
    if K == 1:
        print(total_inv % MOD)
        return
    # build BIT for first K
    bit = [0]*(N+1)
    bit_arr = bit
    for idx in range(K):
        v = P[idx]
        while v <= n:
            bit_arr[v] += 1
            v += v & -v
    inv = first_window_inv
    S = inv
    M = N - K + 1
    # slide M-1 times
    # Need local P, bit_arr, n, K
    for s in range(M - 1):
        v = P[s]
        # count less than v in current window
        idx = v - 1
        cnt = 0
        while idx > 0:
            cnt += bit_arr[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv -= cnt
        # remove v
        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit_arr[idx] -= 1
            idx += idx & -idx
        v2 = P[s + K]
        # count <= v2 in current window (after removal), length K-1
        idx = v2
        cnt = 0
        while idx > 0:
            cnt += bit_arr[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv += (K - 1) - cnt
        # add v2
        idx = v2
        while idx <= n:
            bit_arr[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx
        S += inv
    ans = (total_inv % MOD + C - (S % MOD) * pow(M, MOD-2, MOD)) % MOD
    print(ans)

Need compute C before? For K==N output C. For general, C = K*(K-1)%MOD*INV4%MOD. We can compute at top after K. For K==1, C=0 but answer total_inv. If K==1 and we return before C? C=0. Fine.

Let's ensure sliding update for inv_inside after remove and add is correct when K=2:
Initial window inv = [P0>P1].
Slide remove P0: inv -= count_less(P0) in window. Window [P0,P1]; count_less(P0) = 1 if P1<P0, i.e., original inversion. So inv becomes 0. Then add P2: inv += count_greater(P2) in [P1] = 1 if P1>P2. New window [P1,P2] inv = [P1>P2]. Good.

For K=3:
Window [a,b,c], inv = [a>b]+[a>c]+[b>c].
Remove a: count_less(a) among {a,b,c} = [b<a]+[c<a] (a not counted). inv -= those, leaves [b>c]. Add d: count_greater(d) among {b,c} = [b>d]+[c>d]. New inv [b>c]+[b>d]+[c>d]. Good.

Let's craft code:

import sys

MOD = 998244353
INV4 = pow(4, MOD - 2, MOD)

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    K = data[1]
    P = data[2:]
    # P length N

    if K == N:
        ans = (K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD * INV4 % MOD
        print(ans)
        return

    bit = [0] * (N + 1)
    total_inv = 0
    first_window_inv = 0
    n = N
    for i, v in enumerate(P):
        s = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        total_inv += i - s

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        if i == K - 1:
            first_window_inv = total_inv

    if K == 1:
        print(total_inv % MOD)
        return

    bit = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i in range(K):
        v = P[i]
        while v <= n:
            bit[v] += 1
            v += v & -v

    inv = first_window_inv
    S = inv
    M = N - K + 1
    K_minus_1 = K - 1

    for s in range(M - 1):
        v = P[s]
        cnt = 0
        idx = v - 1
        while idx:
            cnt += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv -= cnt

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] -= 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        v = P[s + K]
        cnt = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            cnt += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv += K_minus_1 - cnt

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        S += inv

    C = (K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD * INV4 % MOD
    invM = pow(M, MOD - 2, MOD)
    ans = (total_inv % MOD + C - (S % MOD) * invM) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's see if we can create a Fenwick class with methods and bind:
class Fenwick:
    def __init__(self,n):
        self.n=n; self.bit=[0]*(n+1)
    def add(self,i,x):
        n=self.n; bit=self.bit
        while i<=n:
            bit[i]+=x; i+=i&-i
    def sum(self,i):
        bit=self.bit; s=0
        while i>0:
            s+=bit[i]; i-=i&-i
        return s
Method calls with attribute lookup. Bind methods:
fw=Fenwick(N); add=fw.add; pref=fw.sum
Then calls local. But methods still access self. Fine.

Full code:

import sys

MOD = 998244353
INV4 = pow(4, MOD - 2, MOD)

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    K = data[1]
    P = data[2:2+N]

    if K == N:
        print((K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD * INV4 % MOD)
        return

    n = N
    bit = [0] * (n + 1)
    total_inv = 0

    # first K elements
    for i in range(K):
        v = P[i]
        s = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        total_inv += i - s
        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

    first_window_inv = total_inv

    # remaining elements
    for i in range(K, N):
        v = P[i]
        s = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        total_inv += i - s
        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

    if K == 1:
        print(total_inv % MOD)
        return

    # prepare BIT for first window
    if K <= N - K:
        bit = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(K):
            v = P[i]
            idx = v
            while idx <= n:
                bit[idx] += 1
                idx += idx & -idx
    else:
        for i in range(K, N):
            v = P[i]
            idx = v
            while idx <= n:
                bit[idx] -= 1
                idx += idx & -idx

    inv = first_window_inv
    S = inv
    M = N - K + 1
    K1 = K - 1

    for s in range(M - 1):
        v = P[s]
        cnt = 0
        idx = v - 1
        while idx:
            cnt += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv -= cnt

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] -= 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        v = P[s + K]
        cnt = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            cnt += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv += K1 - cnt

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        S += inv

    C = (K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD * INV4 % MOD
    invM = pow(M, MOD - 2, MOD)
    ans = (total_inv % MOD + C - (S % MOD) * invM) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Let's review the inlined code line by line for syntax:

import sys

MOD = 998244353
INV4 = pow(4, MOD - 2, MOD)

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    K = data[1]
    P = data[2:2+N]

    if K == N:
        print((K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD * INV4 % MOD)
        return

    n = N
    bit = [0] * (n + 1)
    total_inv = 0

    for i in range(K):
        v = P[i]
        s = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        total_inv += i - s

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

    first_window_inv = total_inv

    for i in range(K, N):
        v = P[i]
        s = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        total_inv += i - s

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

    if K == 1:
        print(total_inv % MOD)
        return

    if K <= N - K:
        bit = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(K):
            v = P[i]
            idx = v
            while idx <= n:
                bit[idx] += 1
                idx += idx & -idx
    else:
        for i in range(K, N):
            v = P[i]
            idx = v
            while idx <= n:
                bit[idx] -= 1
                idx += idx & -idx

    inv = first_window_inv
    S = inv
    M = N - K + 1
    K1 = K - 1

    for s in range(M - 1):
        v = P[s]
        cnt = 0
        idx = v - 1
        while idx:
            cnt += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv -= cnt

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] -= 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        v = P[s + K]
        cnt = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            cnt += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv += K1 - cnt

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        S += inv

    C = (K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD * INV4 % MOD
    invM = pow(M, MOD - 2, MOD)
    ans = (total_inv % MOD + C - (S % MOD) * invM) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

ans = ((K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD) * INV4 % MOD

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if K == N:" and then `print((K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD * INV4 % MOD)`; operator precedence: `%` and `*` left
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353
INV4 = pow(4, MOD - 2, MOD)


def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]
    K = data[1]

    if K == N:
        print(((K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD) * INV4 % MOD)
        return

    P = data[2:2 + N]
    del data

    n = N
    bit = [0] * (n + 1)
    total_inv = 0

    # inversions inside the first window, and total inversions
    for i in range(K):
        v = P[i]
        s = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        total_inv += i - s

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

    first_window_inv = total_inv

    for i in range(K, N):
        v = P[i]
        s = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            s += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        total_inv += i - s

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

    if K == 1:
        print(total_inv % MOD)
        return

    # Prepare Fenwick tree for the first window.
    if K <= N - K:
        bit = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(K):
            v = P[i]
            idx = v
            while idx <= n:
                bit[idx] += 1
                idx += idx & -idx
    else:
        for i in range(K, N):
            v = P[i]
            idx = v
            while idx <= n:
                bit[idx] -= 1
                idx += idx & -idx

    inv = first_window_inv
    sum_window_inv = inv
    M = N - K + 1
    K1 = K - 1

    # Sum inversion counts of all sliding windows of length K.
    for s in range(M - 1):
        v = P[s]

        cnt = 0
        idx = v - 1
        while idx:
            cnt += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv -= cnt

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] -= 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        v = P[s + K]

        cnt = 0
        idx = v
        while idx:
            cnt += bit[idx]
            idx -= idx & -idx
        inv += K1 - cnt

        idx = v
        while idx <= n:
            bit[idx] += 1
            idx += idx & -idx

        sum_window_inv += inv

    C = ((K % MOD) * ((K - 1) % MOD) % MOD) * INV4 % MOD
    invM = pow(M, MOD - 2, MOD)

    ans = (total_inv % MOD + C - (sum_window_inv % MOD) * invM) % MOD
    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
